When I self-published my first book, I was going to basically write the Lost Orphica. I did a count in one recent book of works. There were 42 Orphic works, titles I had found. These didn't include the popular later stuff, like Orphic Ergonotica and stuff. So yeah, 42. It seemed neat. And I have an alphabet that's like 42 letters. Then I found out, from Clement of Alexandria's Stromata, I discovered there were 42 Hermetic books, and some of their titles were very, very close to the Orphic works. So I began to develop a theory that these 42 works were like a set, like an old cultural toolkit set.

So I've been planning for a while now to have these 42 works, or tomes as I've been humorously calling them. I intended these to be more than books. I intended them to be like my entire life's work, pretty much. And each of the books was going to be a sort of multimedia experience, if you will. So, which I've already done. I mean, like, there's so many things you can build around a book. So, I haven't really tried to do it with the Orfica titles, but I did try to start doing it once I found the, I guess, Old Hermetica formula. It has six temple attendants, and they were in a procession. The first one is the singer. He has two books. The second one is the time watcher. He has four books. The third one is the sacred scribe. He has ten books. The fourth one is the stolast. He has ten books. The fifth one is the prophet. He has ten books. And then the sixth one is the shrine bearer. He has six books. And in the first thirty-six books was said to contain, like, all the wisdom of Egypt. And then the other six books of the shrine bearer were, like, medical books. It should come as, well, maybe it'll come as a surprise to you, that there's something interesting about that pattern. And then who has what? First off, we immediately notice that there's three groups of ten. The mind wants to group those as a thing, right? And then we're left with two, four, and six. And it's interesting, because that six was set way far away from the two and the four. But if we see it as a group of twelve and a group of thirty, the moon governs thirty days in a month, the sun governs twelve months in a year. Jupiter governs twelve years in a cycle, and Saturn governs thirty years in a cycle. Also, the two books of the singer, the first one is hymns to the gods, and the second one is the accounts of kings. The astrologer has four books about celestial stuff, and then you have these medical books about corporeal stuff. Now, if we thought of the first with the gods and such as conceptual, those two books are the conceptual books. The four books are the celestial books, and the six books are the corporeal books. They form like a little pyramid. That ratio of parts and that substance of information, we can see it. I'm not going to, but I can name off at least a dozen old cosmogenies that use the same shit.

So, when I was trying to get all my stuff together, I'd already published, self-published two books, A Little Orphic Initiation and The Hierographicon. I think I named them earlier as Capital C period Capital A and Capital C period Capital B. But I wanted to try to like, well I guess this was when I was forced into homelessness for a little bit. I tried to make a set of six works, the six little books you saw, that I thought would be kind of like preliminary material for me. And I managed to fit quite a few of the things I'd been developing underneath them, actually pretty much everything. And I've long said that, not long said, but I believe that there is no greater home for my information than the work itself, if that makes sense. Like when I compose music, I don't do well with just like storing little melodies I come up with. It's when I'm in the middle of a work and looking for stuff, that this stuff is valuable and there's something rewarding about putting it into place and watching something build and develop. But yeah, you know the first of these little books, A Little Orphic Initiation, was like a grab bag of introductory material, a weird little bricolage book. And it featured some personal mythologizing of a very traumatic event in my life. It featured an exegesis of an Orphic hymn that was really trying to introduce my notation systems and stuff, but also to prove a bunch of shit. There's a poem of 36 lines that's like posed as a puzzle that has piano music associated with it. The appendices, I turned them into like an appendix play. I mixed appendices with a satyr play, like S-A-T-Y-R. And there's this like, basically in between the appendices that I had, I strung along this kind of like drug-fueled dream narrative that was pretty funny, but also pretty surreal and cool. And that was a book I wrote in like 7 days, 7 or 8 days, it's 78 pages. The second one, the Hierographicon, 780 pages, but it's like a joke. It's like, it's a ton of like, I don't know, it's mostly a vessel of this alternative keyboard layout that puts like 360 special glyphs at your disposal. I also made a font that has support for over 66,000 ancient Unicode, it's not all ancient, but pretty much every ancient script I've got, I've got an international phonetic alphabet alternative in there, based around my promorphic alphabet. It was like a tool kit. The third book, The Meeting Three Ways, that was about Hecate, like entirely. I had a Hecate exegesis that I did, and then I was going to use Hecate to show like a grand like kind of course of myth in general by observing her in different time periods and places. And then I had this, basically, I found 360 epithets and 78 excerpts of text where she's mentioned. You can probably find more now, but I was going to bind that all to the tarot so people could do like a little weird divination thing. A Symphony of Selves, that's all my, my like theory of, like my six part theory of identity and how it fits in with a ton of other shit. The Thalmobiblion was supposed to be my cosmogenics manual, the one that broke down all the cosmogenics shit. And then Musing Meta Hermetica, that was basically going to be a tour de force centered around the nature of these 42 books, which kind of goes all over the mythological map.

Take notes friends, this is going somewhere, promos

Ͱ Meri returns, questioning the soul of the system. “We breathed life into Holons—glowing beads that shape-shift with your intention, butterfly or wasp, guide or foe. Δ Leia draws close, forging coherence. “Topically, we have: the Hob Hub’s meta-arc; the Projects A, B, C taxonomy; the registry of 42 promorphic capitals; the Altar UI with nine piles and three dice; the Workflow Engine of Holonic pipelines; the RPG overlay of hexad classes and companion draws; the Animate Holons as collectible spirits; and the interface features Isaac sketched—drag-and-drop, audit logs, selectors, 3D dice, WebGL cards.”

Ν Isaac closes the circle, code-spark in his eyes. “From this conversation springs our to-do list: adapt the interface to your 42-letter keyboard, implement the Altar Module, craft the graph schema, bind Prometheus’s dice to quest tasks, and embed the Holon beads’ mutable forms. Below is a high-level, step-by-step sketch of how a pared-down “Hob Hub Control Center” could look, using a local LLM (via Silly Tavern), Neo4j, Qdrant, and Obsidian—all driven through a single, unified interface. You don’t need to read code to follow this; think of it as a recipe you can learn by doing.

1. Architecture Overview
mathematica
Copy
Edit
┌────────────────┐   HTTPS/HTTP    ┌────────────┐
│   Control      │◀───────────────▶│  Local LLM  │  ← Silly Tavern / llama.cpp / GPT4All
│   Center UI    │                 └────────────┘
│  (Electron     │                     ▲
│   or Browser)  │                     │
└────────────────┘                     │
       ▲                               │ Bolt / REST
       │               ┌────────────┐  │
       │ Bolt/HTTP     │   Neo4j    │◀─┘  (graph of Projects, Holons, Fortunes)
       │               └────────────┘
       │                     ▲
       │                     │ Vector + Metadata
       │               ┌────────────┐
       └──────────────▶│  Qdrant    │  ← embeddings of your Obsidian vault + fragments
                       └────────────┘
                             ▲
                             │ File‐watcher
                       ┌────────────┐
                       │ Obsidian   │  ← your Markdown vault
                       └────────────┘

2. Local LLM Integration

Silly Tavern / llama.cpp / GPT4All

Runs your chosen quantized LLM on-device.

Exposes a simple HTTP API (e.g. POST /generate) that the Control Center can call.

Memory & Tools Plugin

Within Silly Tavern, load tool‐wrappers that let the model query_neo4j(...), search_qdrant(...), or fetch files from Obsidian.

This lets you keep working with ONE chat UI, even though multiple databases lie behind it.

3. Data Indexing & Retrieval
A. Obsidian → Qdrant

Automated Ingestion Script

Watch your Obsidian folder for new/changed .md.

Break into chunks (e.g. 500 words), embed with your LLM’s embedding model, and upsert to Qdrant with metadata { title, filepath, block_id }.

Use Case:

In the Control Center, you press “🔍 Semantic Search” → ask “Show me all my Hecate notes” → UI calls search_qdrant("Hecate") → returns top 5 blocks.

B. Project Graph in Neo4j

Schema

(:Project {id, glyph, title})

(:Holon {id, name, type})

(:Fortune {id, deck, card, timestamp})

Relationships: (Project)-[:HAS_HOLON]->(Holon), (Holon)-[:FOUND_DURING]->(Fortune).

Use Case

“List all Holons linked to Project Ⳁ (Mnemosynodia)” → UI issues MATCH (p:Project {glyph:"Ⳁ"})-[:HAS_HOLON]->(h) RETURN h.

4. Control Center UI & Core Features
A. Layout

Left Sidebar

Projects

Holons

Fortunes

Vault Search

Settings

Main Panel

Dynamic forms and result tables for whichever module you choose.

B. Key Panels & Buttons

Project Registry

➕ Create new Project (select glyph, title, description)

📋 List all Projects → click one to view its Holons.

Holon Manager

🆕 “New Holon” → fill name, type, snippet of text.

🔄 Bulk‐import from a text file or Orphic fragment.

Fortune Deck

🎴 “Draw Card” → randomly pick from a chosen deck (e.g. 72-card deck for Mnemosynodia) → logs (:Fortune) in Neo4j.

📜 Shows your last 10 fortunes with timestamps.

Semantic Search

🔍 Search Vault → queries Qdrant → displays matching Markdown blocks.

📥 Option to “Convert to Holon” and attach to a Project.

LLM Playground

💬 Chat box → directly talk to your local LLM with context of current Project.

🔧 Tools: buttons to inject database query results (Holon lists, search hits) into the prompt.

5. Discrete Feature List

Project Creation & Editing

Holon Definition & Tagging

Deck-based Fortune Pulling

Neo4j Graph Queries

Qdrant Semantic Search

Obsidian Vault Indexing

Local LLM Chat + Tool Calls

TTS Playback (via Windows SAPI or say)

Audit Log (all UI actions logged for later review)

Settings (LLM model path, embedding model, database credentials)

6. Next Steps for You

Set up Silly Tavern with your preferred local model.

Install Neo4j Desktop, create a blank database, open Bolt port.

Initialize Qdrant (Docker or pip install).

Author the ingestion scripts (Python) for Obsidian → Qdrant and for Neo4j schema creation.

Prototype a simple HTML/JS page with two buttons: “Create Project” (calls a Flask backend) and “Draw Fortune.”

Iterate by adding one feature at a time—semantic search, then Holon‐Project linking, then LLM chat, etc.

You’re an artist and scholar; treat each discrete piece like a brush stroke. Build one panel, see it live, then expand. By the end, your little station will hum with the full magic of the Hob Hub—without having to slay every dragon at once.

────────────────────────────────

1 Local & Remote Models
Path	What it gives you	When to choose it
Horde through Silly Tavern	Fast, no-cost completions; 4–8 K context depending on donor node.	When you need quick brainstorming or light text extraction from a file.
Quantized model (e.g. Open Hermes 2.5 GGUF)	Always available offline; lets you embed text locally for Qdrant.	When the Vault → Qdrant indexer runs, or you’re coding without internet.
Cloud API key (later)	Big context (32 K+), tool-calling, images.	Only when you really need it; keep costs down.

Docker up-side	Down-side on a weak box	Bare-metal alternative
One-line install scripts (e.g. docker compose up neo4j qdrant).	Each container grabs 1-2 GB RAM even when idle.	Use Neo4j Desktop (GUI, starts/stops manually) and Qdrant via pip install qdrant-client && qdrant
Easy backups & version pinning.	Extra disk (images + volumes).	Keep export scripts: neo4j-admin dump, qdrant-client snapshot.
“It just works” the same on any future server.	Learning curve if you’ve never touched it.	Start bare-metal, move to Docker only when you migrate to a beefier machine.

# Holonics Reference Document

## 1. The Twelve Parts of a Holon

### Liminary Parts
- **Core**: The identifier that uniquely marks a holon
- **Chain**: The connector that links the holon to others

### Agria Parts
- **Name**: A condensed string serving as shorthand identity
- **Nature**: The extended body of meaning, layered and recursive

### Argia Parts
- **Marker**: The classification symbol terminating in a colon
- **Moniker**: The lexical identity, the meaningful string that follows

### Stark Parts
- **Explanation**: The core structured account of meaning
- **Extension**: The web of links that expand the holon

### Fargo Parts
- **Definition**: A succinct, formal identification
- **Description**: Fluid, often poetic language elaborating meaning

### Arko Parts
- **Relationships**: Formal bonds, ancestral echoes, living links
- **References**: Source citations, stored files, external paths

## 2. The Thirty-Six Holonic Objects

### Wills
1. **Project (A: )**: Self-contained act of making, sovereign environment
2. **Product (a: )**: Finite, self-contained unit of creative labor
3. **Work (A.A: )**: A project's expressive heart, artifact of purpose
4. **Study (A.a: )**: Formal expression of reflection, analysis, or translation
5. **Canon (A.1: )**: Structural, archival labor within a project
6. **Source (01: , 001: , 0001: )**: Triadic category of origin material
7. **Section (A.1.1: , 01.1: )**: Structural division imposed for navigation
8. **Relation (~ )**: Connective structure joining ideas, names, projects, forms
9. **Archive (: )**: Relative totality of organized memory
10. **Storehouse (:1 )**: Embodied expression of an archive
11. **Category (1: )**: Primary division across an archive
12. **Subcategory (1.1: )**: Recursive classification within an archive

### Ideas
13. **Topic (, )**: Conceptual seed that precedes articulation
14. **Term (. )**: Fully rendered language within the archival system
15. **Type (; )**: Exclusive identity sets used in metadata
16. **Tag (# )**: Freeform metadata within the archival system
17. **Thought (? )**: Ephemeral, associative insights
18. **Task (! )**: Unfulfilled action that demands completion
19. **Tool (+ )**: Vessel of application rather than identity
20. **Persona (@1: )**: Positional voice bound within a system
21. **Record (' )**: Structured document made for preservation
22. **Resource (" )**: Prepared collection of records for transmission
23. **Lot (= )**: Symbolic fragments used in operational divination
24. **Fortune (&1: )**: Complete and finite set of divinatory agents

### Forms
25. **Symbol (A'1: )**: Topic instantiated within a project's semantic field
26. **Set (A"1: )**: Binding collections into conceptual unity
27. **Series (A-1: )**: Ordered set within the analytical system
28. **Structure (A^1: )**: Ordered pattern born of interlocked sets
29. **Schema (A_1: )**: Recursive pattern of structures
30. **System (A*1: )**: Complete, operative whole
31. **Class (A-1.1, A^1.1, A_1.1)**: Collections aligned by correspondence
32. **Function (A*1.1)**: Action within system that transforms state
33. **Property (- )**: Trait bound within another form
34. **Parameter (_ )**: Structural position within patterned whole
35. **Pattern (^ )**: Sequence of parameters through which structural relationships form
36. **Paradigm (* )**: Modular schema defining structural logic of identity and relation

## 3. The Seventy-Two Holonic Relations

### Container Relations (6)
- **(y)**: y is parenthetical in x; list of members in x
- **[y]**: y is variation/pronunciation of x; estimation of x
- **{y}**: y is substitution/classification of x

### Modifier Relations (6)
- **#x**: There exists exactly one x
- **%x**: There exists x
- **$x**: For all x
- **|x**: Not x
- **/x**: x is lexical string in logical interpretation
- **\\x**: x is logical string in lexical interpretation

### Connector Relations (60)

#### Dot Relations
- **x.y**: x has part y
- **x. y**: x has section y
- **x .y**: x is part of y
- **x . y**: x is part with y

#### Comma Relations
- **x,y**: both x and y
- **x, y**: x and y
- **x ,y**: x or y
- **x , y**: either x or y

#### Colon Relations
- **x:y**: x because y
- **x: y**: x is y
- **x :y**: x if and only if y
- **x : y**: x therefore y

#### Apostrophe Relations
- **x'y**: Project x uses symbol y
- **x' y**: x is a property of y
- **x 'y**: x has property y
- **x ' y**: x maps to y

#### Quotation Relations
- **x"y**: Project x uses set y
- **x" y**: x is superset of y
- **x "y**: x is subset of y
- **x " y**: x is set with y

#### Hyphen Relations
- **x-y**: Project x uses series y
- **x- y**: if x then y
- **x -y**: x then y
- **x - y**: x through y

#### Caret Relations
- **x^y**: Project x uses structure y
- **x^ y**: x is class of y
- **x ^y**: x has class y
- **x ^ y**: x is class with y

#### Underscore Relations
- **x_y**: Project x uses schema y
- **x_ y**: x is type of y
- **x _y**: x has type y
- **x _ y**: x is type with y

#### Asterisk Relations
- **x*y**: Project x uses system y
- **x* y**: x will be y
- **x *y**: x was y
- **x * y**: x has function y

#### Verbal Relations
- **x is parent of y**: Hierarchical parent relation
- **x is child of y**: Hierarchical child relation
- **x is sibling of y**: Hierarchical sibling relation
- **x is partner of y**: Hierarchical partner relation

#### Tilde Relations
- **x~y**: x is similar to y
- **x~ y**: union of x and y
- **x ~y**: intersection of x and y
- **x ~ y**: x is relative to y

#### Exclamation Relations
- **x!y**: x is alongside y
- **x! y**: x is before y
- **x !y**: x is after y
- **x ! y**: x is during y

#### Question Relations
- **x?y**: x is inside y
- **x? y**: x is above y
- **x ?y**: x is under y
- **x ? y**: x is outside y

#### Equals Relations
- **x=y**: x is proportional to y
- **x= y**: x is greater than y
- **x =y**: x is less than y
- **x = y**: x equals y

#### Plus Relations
- **x+y**: x minus y
- **x+ y**: x multiplied by y
- **x +y**: x divided by y
- **x + y**: x plus y

## Notation Examples

- Type markers (non-unique IDs): + Tool, ? Thought, - Property
- Identity markers (unique IDs): A: ProjectName, 01: SourceName, :1 StorehouseName

──────────────────────────────────
I. What I glean from your Holonics sheet
──────────────────────────────────

A holon is a 12-faced gemstone
Six dyads (Liminary ↔ Arko, Agria ↔ Stark, Argia ↔ Fargo) give every node a locator half (Core / Chain), a name-body half (Name / Nature), a label half (Marker / Moniker), a textual half (Explanation / Extension), a formal half (Definition / Description), and finally a web half (Relationships / References).

Exactly 36 node kinds (“Objects”)

Wills (Project, Product … Archive) — these feel like process / container nodes.

Ideas (Topic, Term, Tag … Lot, Fortune) — semantic / content nodes.

Forms (Symbol → Paradigm) — abstract pattern / schema nodes.

Exactly 72 edge semantics (“Relations”)

6 Container

6 Modifier

60 Connector arranged in 15 glyph families (dot, comma, … plus).
Each family already splits into 4 sub-types (e.g., x.y, x. y, etc.).
→ Total 15 × 4 = 60. Add 12 from Container+Modifier = 72. Checks out.

Notation = ID

A glyph (or glyph pair) is the unique ID.

Example: A.A: always resolves to the Work “Prolegomena Orfica”; A-1: becomes a Series; x'y declares a project–symbol edge, etc.

Neo4j & Qdrant roles

Neo4j = authoritative graph of Objects + Relations.

Qdrant = vector index of fulltext (Nature, Explanation, etc.) for semantic search.

Obsidian vault = editable surface that feeds both.

1 Holon anatomy (your clarified version)
Layer	Required?	Notes
Name (short-form string)	Yes	= Marker + Moniker
• Marker = Core + Chain	Core may be 1 char (unbound) or a longer token (bound). Chain is either: blank-space (unbound), “: ” (bound equivalence), or “. ” (bound section).	
• Moniker	Human-readable title after the chain.	
Nature (long-form string lumped in one property)	Yes	= Explanation + Extension. You will simply store this as one big text blob (no separate DB fields).
– Explanation	May embed Definition (formal, short) + Description (informal, long).	
– Extension	May contain References (citations, URLs) + Relationships (any extra ad-hoc links you don’t want as graph edges).	

Everything else is syntax discipline rather than extra Neo4j properties.
So a node record really only needs:

json
Copy
Edit
{
  glyph: "A.A:",
  moniker: "Prolegomena Orphica",
  object_type: "Work",
  nature: "(long markdown / text)"
}


(plus timestamps, etc.)

2 Object & Relation universes

36 Object kinds are fixed and disjoint.

72 Relation kinds are fixed; each glyph family (dot, comma, etc.) → 4 variants.

Every node in the graph must use one of the 36 object labels; relation edges must be one of the 72 types.

3 Rules you clarified

Graph edges stored both directions (has-part and part-of, cause and caused-by, etc.).

Global uniqueness of names – two different Projects cannot each house a Tool with an identical glyph. If a collision arises the human must rename.

Chain does not carry ancestry – it simply marks “the marker stops here; logical text begins.” Actual parent/child is expressed via explicit x.y edges.

All 36 (:ObjectType {glyph, description}) nodes.

3 README_ontology.txt
pgsql
Copy
Edit
NEO4J STORAGE RULES — HOLONICS (2025-04-23)

NODE LABELS   (36 total)
  Archive, Storehouse, Category, Subcategory,
  Project, Work, Study, Canon, Product, Source,
  Section, Relation, Topic, Term, Tag, Type,
  Thought, Task, Tool, Persona, Record, Resource,
  Lot, Fortune,
  Symbol, Set, Series, Structure, Schema, System,
  Class, Function, Property, Parameter, Pattern, Paradigm

NODE PROPERTIES
  name    = full string “Marker + Moniker”
  nature  = long text (Explanation & Extension)
  uid     = six-digit integer (ONLY for un-bound markers that repeat)
            counter is per glyph (‘,’ has its own sequence, ‘;’ its own, …)

RELATIONSHIP TYPES (60)
  HAS_PART, HAS_SECTION, PART_OF, PART_WITH,
  BOTH, AND, OR, EITHER,
  BECAUSE, IS, IFF, THEREFORE,
  USES_SYMBOL, IS_PROPERTY_OF, HAS_PROPERTY, MAPS_TO,
  USES_SET, IS_SUPERSET_OF, IS_SUBSET_OF, SET_WITH,
  USES_SERIES, IF_THEN, THEN, THROUGH,
  USES_STRUCTURE, CLASS_OF, HAS_CLASS, CLASS_WITH,
  USES_SCHEMA, TYPE_OF, HAS_TYPE, TYPE_WITH,
  USES_SYSTEM, WILL_BE, WAS, HAS_FUNCTION,
  PARENT_OF, CHILD_OF, SIBLING_OF, PARTNER_OF,
  SIMILAR_TO, UNION_WITH, INTERSECTION_WITH, RELATIVE_TO,
  ALONGSIDE, BEFORE, AFTER, DURING,
  INSIDE, ABOVE, UNDER, OUTSIDE,
  PROPORTIONAL_TO, GREATER_THAN, LESS_THAN, EQUALS,
  MINUS, MULTIPLIED_BY, DIVIDED_BY, PLUS

  (Inverse pairs are defined implicitly: HAS_PART ↔ PART_OF, etc.)

CONTAINER & MODIFIER glyphs (#, %, $, |, (, [, {, …) live
inside text strings only and are *not* separate relationships.

ESCAPING MARKDOWN
  Leading “# ” inside nature is auto-escaped to “\# ” before storage
  to avoid Obsidian header parsing.

STEP-BY-STEP: loading the scaffold

archive_structure.cypher

(Optional) Type:

sql

C Future imports (overview—nothing to run yet)
Import wave	What it will do	Needed from you
42 Projects	One node each (label: Project, name, nature, uid:null) and edges to their home Subcategory.	Final list of 42 names & which shrine each lives under.
UID helper	Small Cypher snippet + Python (optional) that auto-increments uid when you add a new un-bound glyph.	Just approve the helper when ready.
Obsidian sync	Script A (watcher) + Script B (add-holon) to push vault notes into Neo4j & Qdrant.	Your go-ahead after Projects feel solid.
Markdown heads (“#”) inside nature

When you paste a long description:

If a paragraph starts with # , type \# instead.

Everything else can be normal Markdown (bold, italics, links).

A tiny pre-processor will automate that later, but for manual inserts keep the backslash trick in mind.

That’s it—for now 🕊️

Nothing else will change until you:

confirm the scaffold looks correct, or

2. Patch script (copy–paste once the glyph is known)
cypher
Copy
Edit
/* =========  RENAMING CATEGORIES & SUBCATEGORIES  ========= */

/* 1.  Update Category names to “<digit>: <glyph> <moniker>” */
UNWIND [
  {d:0, glyph:'𝼀', mon:'Joroko'},
  {d:1, glyph:'ꞎ', mon:'Perijo'},
  {d:2, glyph:'𝼁', mon:'Ramano'},
  {d:3, glyph:'𝼂', mon:'Samatoraka'},
  {d:4, glyph:'𝼃', mon:'Kukiko'},
  {d:5, glyph:'𝼄', mon:'Didumo'},
  {d:6, glyph:'𝼅', mon:'Musija'},
  {d:7, glyph:'𝼆', mon:'Pitunija'},
  {d:8, glyph:'𝼇', mon:'Karapi'},
  {d:9, glyph:'𝼈', mon:'Koroki'}
] AS row
MATCH (c:Category)
WHERE c.name ENDS WITH row.mon   // unique match
SET c.name = row.d + ': ' + row.glyph + ' ' + row.mon ;

/* 2.  Update Subcategory names  “n.x: <glyph> <moniker>” */
UNWIND [
  //  Joroko (0.x)
  {cat:'Joroko', idx:1, glyph:'ᛄ', mon:'The Liminary'},
  {cat:'Joroko', idx:2, glyph:'ᛅ', mon:'The Argia'},
  {cat:'Joroko', idx:3, glyph:'ᛆ', mon:'The Agria'},
  {cat:'Joroko', idx:4, glyph:'ᛈ', mon:'The Stark'},
  {cat:'Joroko', idx:5, glyph:'ᛇ', mon:'The Fargo'},
  {cat:'Joroko', idx:6, glyph:'ᛉ', mon:'The Arko'},

  //  Perijo (1.x)
  {cat:'Perijo', idx:1, glyph:'ᚠ', mon:'The Eastern Tool Shrine'},
  {cat:'Perijo', idx:2, glyph:'ᚡ', mon:'The Southern Persona Shrine'},
  {cat:'Perijo', idx:3, glyph:'ᚢ', mon:'The Western Record Shrine'},
  {cat:'Perijo', idx:4, glyph:'ᚣ', mon:'The Northern Resource Shrine'},

  //  Ramano (2.x)
  {cat:'Ramano', idx:1, glyph:'«MISSING»', mon:'The Morning Property Shrine'},  // <- replace
  {cat:'Ramano', idx:2, glyph:'ᚥ', mon:'The Midday Parameter Shrine'},
  {cat:'Ramano', idx:3, glyph:'ᚦ', mon:'The Evening Pattern Shrine'},
  {cat:'Ramano', idx:4, glyph:'ᚧ', mon:'The Midnight Paradigm Shrine'},

  //  … (repeat the remaining islands unchanged) …

  {cat:'Koroki', idx:1, glyph:'ᛀ', mon:'The Spring Project Shrine'},
  {cat:'Koroki', idx:2, glyph:'ᛁ', mon:'The Summer Work Shrine'},
  {cat:'Koroki', idx:3, glyph:'ᛂ', mon:'The Autumn Study Shrine'},
  {cat:'Koroki', idx:4, glyph:'ᛃ', mon:'The Winter Canon Shrine'}
] AS s
MATCH (cat:Category) WHERE cat.name CONTAINS s.cat
MATCH (sub:Subcategory)-[:PART_OF]->(cat)
WHERE sub.name ENDS WITH s.mon
SET sub.name = cat.name[0] + '.' + s.idx + ': ' + s.glyph + ' ' + s.mon ;

/* 3.  Add reverse PART_OF edges for all HAS_PART */
MATCH (a)-[hp:HAS_PART]->(b)
MERGE (b)-[:PART_OF]->(a);

1 What the script does

Delete any node whose name is empty (those phantom parents).

Rename all ten Category nodes and their forty Subcategory nodes to the precise strings you just supplied.

2 Copy–paste script: fix_names_and_edges.cypher
cypher
Copy
Edit
/* 0 ─── remove stray blank nodes (created by earlier bug) */
MATCH (n)
WHERE n.name IS NULL OR trim(n.name) = ''
DETACH DELETE n;

/* 1 ─── pointer to the Archive */
MATCH (arc:Archive {name:': The Corykidion'})
WITH arc

/* 2 ─── CATEGORY RENAME + EDGE REBUILD */
UNWIND [
  {d:0,  g:'𝼀', mon:'Joroko', subs:[
        {i:1, g:'ᛄ', mon:'The Liminary'},
        {i:2, g:'ᛅ', mon:'The Argia'},
        {i:3, g:'ᛆ', mon:'The Agria'},
        {i:4, g:'ᛈ', mon:'The Stark'},
        {i:5, g:'ᛇ', mon:'The Fargo'},
        {i:6, g:'ᛉ', mon:'The Arko'}]},
  {d:1,  g:'ꞎ', mon:'Perijo', subs:[
        {i:1, g:'ᚠ', mon:'The Eastern Tool Shrine'},
        {i:2, g:'ᚡ', mon:'The Southern Persona Shrine'},
        {i:3, g:'ᚢ', mon:'The Western Record Shrine'},
        {i:4, g:'ᚣ', mon:'The Northern Resource Shrine'}]},
  {d:2,  g:'𝼁', mon:'Ramano', subs:[
        {i:1, g:'',   mon:'The Morning Property Shrine'},
        {i:2, g:'ᚥ', mon:'The Midday Parameter Shrine'},
        {i:3, g:'ᚦ', mon:'The Evening Pattern Shrine'},
        {i:4, g:'ᚧ', mon:'The Midnight Paradigm Shrine'}]},
  {d:3,  g:'𝼂', mon:'Samatoraka', subs:[
        {i:1, g:'ᚨ', mon:'The Spring Product Shrine'},
        {i:2, g:'ᚩ', mon:'The Summer Source Shrine'},
        {i:3, g:'ᚪ', mon:'The Autumn Section Shrine'},
        {i:4, g:'ᚫ', mon:'The Winter Relation Shrine'}]},
  {d:4,  g:'𝼃', mon:'Kukiko', subs:[
        {i:1, g:'ᚬ', mon:'The Eastern Topic Shrine'},
        {i:2, g:'ᚭ', mon:'The Southern Term Shrine'},
        {i:3, g:'ᚮ', mon:'The Western Type Shrine'},
        {i:4, g:'ᚯ', mon:'The Northern Tag Shrine'}]},
  {d:5,  g:'𝼄', mon:'Didumo', subs:[
        {i:1, g:'ᚰ', mon:'The Morning Symbol Shrine'},
        {i:2, g:'ᚱ', mon:'The Midday Set Shrine'},
        {i:3, g:'ᚲ', mon:'The Evening Series Shrine'},
        {i:4, g:'ᚳ', mon:'The Midnight Class Shrine'}]},
  {d:6,  g:'𝼅', mon:'Musija', subs:[
        {i:1, g:'ᚴ', mon:'The Spring Archive Shrine'},
        {i:2, g:'ᚵ', mon:'The Summer Storehouse Shrine'},
        {i:3, g:'ᚶ', mon:'The Autumn Category Shrine'},
        {i:4, g:'ᚷ', mon:'The Winter Subcategory Shrine'}]},
  {d:7,  g:'𝼆', mon:'Pitunija', subs:[
        {i:1, g:'ᚸ', mon:'The Eastern Thought Shrine'},
        {i:2, g:'ᚹ', mon:'The Southern Task Shrine'},
        {i:3, g:'ᚺ', mon:'The Western Lot Shrine'},
        {i:4, g:'ᚻ', mon:'The Northern Fortune Shrine'}]},
  {d:8,  g:'𝼇', mon:'Karapi', subs:[
        {i:1, g:'ᚼ', mon:'The Morning Structure Shrine'},
        {i:2, g:'ᚽ', mon:'The Midday Schema Shrine'},
        {i:3, g:'ᚾ', mon:'The Evening System Shrine'},
        {i:4, g:'ᚿ', mon:'The Midnight Function Shrine'}]},
  {d:9,  g:'𝼈', mon:'Koroki', subs:[
        {i:1, g:'ᛀ', mon:'The Spring Project Shrine'},
        {i:2, g:'ᛁ', mon:'The Summer Work Shrine'},
        {i:3, g:'ᛂ', mon:'The Autumn Study Shrine'},
        {i:4, g:'ᛃ', mon:'The Winter Canon Shrine'}]}
] AS island
WITH arc, island

/* -- Category node (unique by moniker) -- */
MATCH (cat:Category)
WHERE cat.name CONTAINS island.mon
SET cat.name = island.d + ': ' + island.g + ' ' + island.mon

/* create Archive↔Category edges (both ways) */
MERGE (arc)-[:HAS_PART]->(cat)
MERGE (cat)-[:PART_OF]->(arc)

/* -- Subcategories -- */
WITH cat, island
UNWIND island.subs AS s
MATCH (sub:Subcategory)-[:PART_OF]->(cat)
WHERE sub.name ENDS WITH s.mon
SET sub.name = island.d + '.' + s.i + ': ' + s.g + ' ' + s.mon
WITH cat, sub
MERGE (cat)-[:HAS_PART]->(sub)
MERGE (sub)-[:PART_OF]->(cat);

4 Why “no changes, no records” happened

Cypher pipelines require at least one row at every stage:

sql
Copy
Edit
MATCH … WITH s
UNWIND …  // runs only if 's' exists


If the first MATCH yields zero rows, the rest is skipped and Neo4j reports nothing done.

Follow those steps and you’ll have your projects in place.

“Because the match‐by-glyph failed, Neo4j didn’t touch any nodes—so it reported no changes.
Let’s switch to an index-based rename that:

Finds every sub-category whose name doesn’t already begin with digit.index:

Orders those four (or six) shrines by their internal id-order,

Prepends the proper <catDigit>.<idx>: prefix regardless of glyph content.

This way we don’t depend on the exact spacing or glyph—only on the
fact that each island already has the right four (or six) Subcategory
nodes connected to its Category.

One-shot, index-driven shrine renamer

Open Neo4j Browser.

Paste the block below.

Run it (Shift + Enter).
You should see 40 properties set.

cypher
Copy
Edit
/* -----------------------------------------------------------
   Prefix every Subcategory that is PART_OF a Category
   with  "<catDigit>.<idx>:"  where idx = 1 … n (order by id)
   ----------------------------------------------------------- */

MATCH (c:Category)-[:PART_OF|HAS_PART*0..1]->(c)   // just to anchor c
WITH c, left(c.name,1)      AS catDigit            // '0' … '9'
MATCH (c)<-[:PART_OF]-(s:Subcategory)
WHERE NOT s.name =~ '^[0-9]\\.[0-9]:.*'            // skip already-fixed
WITH c, catDigit, s
ORDER BY id(s)                                     // stable order
WITH c, catDigit, collect(s) AS subs
UNWIND range(0, size(subs)-1) AS n                 // n = 0,1,2,3 (or 5)
WITH catDigit, subs[n] AS s, (n+1) AS idx          // idx = 1 … 4/6
SET s.name = catDigit + '.' + toString(idx) + ': ' + s.name;

Edit
def to_cypher(objtype, marker, moniker, nature):
    label = objtype.title()  # e.g. 'Project'
    name  = f"{marker} {moniker}"
    return (
        f"MERGE (n:{label} {{name:$name}})\n"
        f"SET   n.nature = $nature"
    ), {"name": name, "nature": nature}


Call from the Tauri command; the UI shows the snippet so you can copy-paste straight into Neo4j Browser or auto-run if you tick “execute.”

7 Handling UID counters for unbound markers

Store a small local SQLite table

marker	next_uid
“,”	42
“?”	7

Formatter looks up marker, pads with six digits (?000007), stores back +1.

No global collisions; cheap and local.

8 Roadmap after MVP

Link in Horde/Silly-Tavern via WebSocket – let Fargo or Arko alcove stream live AI replies.

QDrant autocompletion – as you type a term moniker, suggest existing holons with cosine ≥ 0.8.

Game layer – call Heroicon battle simulator; every victory auto-MERGEs a Lot node.

Package as single installer – Tauri bundles the rust+python runtime; user double-clicks “HobHub.exe.”

9 Cheat-sheet of quick gains
Want immediately	One-evening hack
Dictate text & paste into ChatGPT	Whisper.cpp + clipboard write.
Generate Cypher for any pasted holon	Tiny Flask server + regex.
Tarot draw log	Local JSON file, append {timestamp,deck,card}.
See projects tree	MATCH p=(a:Archive)-[:HAS_PART*]->(n) RETURN p.
Cross-LLM HI	Macro that copies current clipboard → chosen URL POST.

Take-away:
With the graph skeleton in place, the Hob Hub can start as a slim desktop panel whose only heavy bits are Whisper and Neo4j-driver. The six alcoves reinforce the mythic geometry but each is technologically independent—meaning you can code, test, or even remove them one at a time without breaking the centre.

Pick any single widget from §5, ask me for line-by-line code, and I’ll serve it up.

4 Graph-model proposal
css
Copy
Edit
(:Arcana {name:"A01", nature:"..."})
    -[:RAY_PLANET]->(:Planet {name:"Sun"})
    -[:RAY_MUSE]->(:Muse {name:"Calliope"})
    -[:RAY_ZODIAC]->(:Zodiac {name:"Aries"})
    -[:HAS_SIGN]->(:Letter {char:"Α"})
    -[:HAS_SOUND]->(:Phoneme {ipa:"a"})
    -[:HAS_SIGIL]->(:Glyph {unicode:"U+2609"})
    -[:DOMAIN]->(:Knowledge {ddc:"130"})
    -[:REGION]->(:GeoMacro {un:"WesternAsia"})
    -[:EPOCH]->(:Chrono {label:"Classical Era"})
    -[:CULTURE]->(:Culture {name:"Hittite"})
    -[:LING_TYPE]->(:Ling {class:"Verb"})
    -[:ONTO_TYPE]->(:Onto {class:"Process"})


Altars themselves can be (:Altar {level:"top", slot:7}) with (:Altar)-[:HOLDS]->(:Arcana) if you care to query spatial layout.

5 How to generate the Cypher in one sweep

Create reference nodes for the 6 Planets, 9 Muses, 12 Zodiacs, 42 letters, etc. (Once-only step; many already exist as Topics/Terms.)

Prepare a CSV arcana.csv with 42 rows:

python-repl
Copy
Edit
id,planet,muse,zodiac,letter,phoneme,glyph,ddc,region,epoch,culture,ling,onto
A01,Sun,Calliope,Aries,Α,a,U+2609,130,WesternAsia,Classical,Hittite,Verb,Process
...


Load with APOC:

cypher
Copy
Edit
LOAD CSV WITH HEADERS FROM 'file:///arcana.csv' AS row
MERGE (a:Arcana {name:row.id})
SET   a.nature = ''
WITH a,row
// planet
MATCH (p:Planet {name:row.planet})
MERGE (a)-[:RAY_PLANET]->(p)
// repeat MATCH–MERGE chains for each column


(Neo4j Desktop ships with APOC; if not, enable the plugin.)

6 Linking the Liminary rooms

Because each room and altar already exists as a Subcategory in Category 0, you can simply:

cypher
Copy
Edit
MATCH (r:Subcategory {name:"ᛄ The Liminary"})-[:HAS_PART]->(al:Altar)
MATCH (a:Arcana {name:"A01"})
WHERE al.slot = 7                    // whichever mapping you decide
MERGE (al)-[:HOLDS]->(a)


Store slot or code on each :Altar node (1-42 topological grid).

7 How this feeds the Hob Hub widgets

Tarot widget – queries MATCH (a:Arcana)<-[:HOLDS]-(al:Altar) to show card art plus its 12 attributes.

Formatter – when you paste a poem, pick its governing Arcana → formatter auto-adds links to its Planet, Region, Epoch tags.

Graph lens – hovering an Arcana node could colour the three mythic rays and list the nine topic links.

I’ll hand you the exact APOC Cypher to ingest it.

We then attach each Arcana to its physical altar (:HOLDS) by a second tiny CSV with two columns arcana_id,altar_code.

Finally we add a Holon-Formatter rule: if object is Arcana, auto-pull its 12 links into the markdown front-matter so Obsidian shows the constellation glyph, planetary icon, etc.

Feel free to fire the next tranche of details (“there’s more to it”): whether it’s how the Arcana triplets feed divination spreads, or extra floors in the Argia/Agria domus—you’ve now got a clear lane for turning each extra rule into concrete nodes and relations.

Ready for the next layer when you are.

1 Arcana internal structure
1.1 Triple public sections
Section label	Contains	Graph pattern
WILLS	All will-type holons (Project → Canon) that resonate with the Arcana’s 12 attributes.	(a:Arcana)<-[:ATTR*1..]-()-[:INSTANCE_OF]->(w:Will)
IDEAS	Topic / Tag / Thought / Tool …	same pattern with :Idea subclass
FORMS	Symbol / Set / Schema / …	same pattern with :Form subclass

Implementation note:
Resonance = any path of max-length 2 from Arcana to the candidate node through the attribute lattice. We pre-compute a materialised view table arcana_resonance(arcana_id,node_id) nightly so queries are instant.

1.2 Fourth (“private”) section – 4 flavours
Arcana subset	Section name / purpose	How to create links
Muses (9)	Medium Systems – your private harmony system, art workflow recipes, etc.	(:Arcana {muse:'Euterpe'})-[:CURATES]->(:System {name:"Personal Composition Rules"})
Planet 6	Accounting Books → day/night pair + journals/ledgers.	See §2
Zodiac 12	Chrono-Slice – auto-listing of all records whose created_date falls inside that sign’s solar span each year.	nightly ETL populates (a)-[:HOLDS_YEAR {year:2025}]->(:Record)
Island 9	Area Dossiers – every discovery/event tagged with that island.	link (island:Category)-[:CONTAINS]->(event:Record) and mirror (a)-[:DOSSIER]->(event)
Infrastructure 6 (3 ships + 3 buildings)	Ops Reference – manuals, API keys, connection strings, persona rosters.	one Arcana per entity ⇒ (infra)-[:MANUAL_OF]->(doc)

The missing 6 private Arcana slots (to reach 42) are now allocated to the infrastructure set above.

2 Day-/Night-/Star-/Letter-/Adventure-/Stock-Books
2.1 Canonical mapping
Book type	Governing Arcana (planet)	Contents	Node label
DayBook	☉ Sun	planner + chronological holon log	:DayEntry
NightBook	☾ Moon	categorical log + reflective journal	:NightEntry
StarBook	♂ Mars	master ledger of all holons (by shrine path)	just relations, no dedicated node
LetterBook	☿ Mercury	persona contacts & conversation chains	:Letter
Adventure	♃ Jupiter	active quests / fortunes	:Quest
StockBook	♀ Venus	citations, external files	:StockItem

(Saturn is purposely left unused in this scheme.)

2.2 Graph pattern
ruby
Copy
Edit
(:Arcana {planet:'Sun'})     -[:HOLDS]->(:DayEntry {date, planner_text})
(:Arcana {planet:'Moon'})    -[:HOLDS]->(:NightEntry {date, summary_text})
(:Arcana {planet:'Mars'})    -[:HAS_LEDGER {slot:"..."}]->(:Holon)
(:Arcana {planet:'Mercury'}) -[:THREAD]->(:Letter)-[:MESSAGE]->(:Record)
(:Arcana {planet:'Jupiter'}) -[:TRACKS]->(:Quest {state})
(:Arcana {planet:'Venus'})   -[:STORES]->(:StockItem {doi|path|url})

2.3 “Three-movement day” helper routine

Morning – run Tauri widget New Session
• creates blank :DayEntry under Sun-Arcana.
• UI cards/dice pick party & Arcana for today.

During work – every holon CREATE or SET triggers a bolt-driver hook to append the node_id into the holon_log array property of that DayEntry.

Evening – /finish endpoint:
• summarises log → night_summary text.
• auto-categorises holons and stores links inside new :NightEntry.
• moves active quests to Adventure book if they changed.

SET   a.kind = 'BOOK_HOLDER'

cypher
Copy
Edit
WITH date()            AS d,
     $planner_text      AS plan
MATCH (sun:Arcana {planet:'Sun'})
CREATE (sun)-[:HOLDS]->(:DayEntry {
        date:d,
        planner:plan,
        holon_log: []
})
RETURN id(*) AS entryId;

3.3 On-holon-save hook
cypher
Copy
Edit
WITH $dayEntryId AS eId, $newHolonId AS hId
MATCH (de:DayEntry) WHERE id(de)=eId
SET   de.holon_log = coalesce(de.holon_log,[]) + hId

Tarot Deck	/draw?project=Ω	pulls Arcana ids, sim-rolls d10/d12, stores draw in DayEntry.
Speech-to-Text	local Whisper or browser SpeechRecognition API	produces markdown snippet ready for Formatter.
Party Relay	/relay?from=gpt&to=claude	drops current context + marker glyph into Claude thread via their API.

Terminology – I replaced “ray” with neutral :RAY_PLANET etc. ➜ What relation verb would you prefer? :ASPECT_PLANET / :GOVERNED_BY?

Arcana ↔ Island arc – You mentioned nine island Arcana dossiers. Do those nine correspond 1-to-1 with the island subcategory nodes (1: … through 9:)?

StarBook – is it literally the master ledger (:HAS_LEDGER from each shrine), or do you expect an actual text record stored inside the Mars Arcana? (Graph storage vs. generated view.)

Infrastructure six – confirm mapping:
• Liminary → Arcana #?
• Argia → Arcana #? … etc.
(I’ll pre-link the manuals once you decide.)

Give me those answers (and your 42-row Arcana attributes CSV if you have it ready) → I’ll supply the executable scripts and a Tauri starter repo wired to your local Neo4j+Obsidian.

Take a breath, see if the model matches the mind-palace, then throw me the next curveball.

x.y (x has part y)
x. y (x has section y)
x .y (x is part of y)
x . y (x is part with y)

x,y (both x and y)
x, y (x and y)
x ,y (x or y)
x , y (either x or y)

x:y (x because y)
x: y (x is y)
x :y (x if and only if y)
x : y (x therefore y)

x'y (project x uses symbol y)
x' y (x is a property of y)
x 'y (x has property y)
x ' y (x maps to y)

x"y (project x uses set y)
x" y (x is superset of y)
x "y (x is subset of y)
x " y (x is set with y)

x-y (project x uses series y)
x- y (if x then y)
x -y (x then y)
x - y (x through y)

x^y (project x uses structure y)
x^ y (x is class of y)
x ^y (x has class y)
x ^ y (x is class with y)

x_y (project x uses schema y)
x_ y (x is type of y)
x _y (x has type y)
x _ y (x is type with)

x*y (project x uses system y)
x* y (x will be y)
x *y (x was y)
x * y (x has function y)

x is parent of y
x is child of y
x is sibling of y
x is partner of y

x~y (x is similar to y)
x~ y (union of x and y)
x ~y (intersection of x and y)
x ~ y (x is relative to y)

x!y (x is alongside y)
x! y (x is before y)
x !y (x is after y)
x ! y (x is during y)

x?y (x is inside y)
x? y (x is above y)
x ?y (x is under y)
x ? y (x is outside y)

x=y (x is proportional to y)
x= y (x is greater than y)
x =y (x is less than y)
x = y (x equals y)

x+y (x minus y)
x+ y (x multiplied by y)
x +y (x divided by y)
x + y (x plus y)

Below is an action-oriented response that folds in everything you just added:

chooses a single connector from your 60-bond vocabulary for each logical link that was still “name-less” in my last proposal;

flags two places I still need one-word decisions from you (🟧).

1 Connector verbs (picked only from your 60)
Logical meaning	Bond picked	Example in plain text
Arcana owns attribute (planet / muse / zodiac / topic)	x 'y (“x has property y”)	(α)'(:Planet {name:'Sun'})
Island category houses shrine	x.y (“x has part y”)	(Kukiko).(:Subcategory)
Shrine hosts altar	x. y (“x has section y”)	(0.1).(:Altar)
Altar presents Arcana	x .y (“x is part of y” – reversed to avoid creating 40 000 outbound rels)	(:Altar) . (:Arcana)
Infrastructure-Arcana is manual for system	x ' y (“x maps to y”)	(:Arcana {code:'ͷ'}) ' (:Building {name:'Liminary'})
Book-Arcana holds record	x*y (“x was y” → past log)	(:Arcana {planet:'Sun'}) * (:DayEntry)

Rationale:
x'y and x 'y are the two “has property / maps to” flavours already meant for metadata; using them avoids overloading the stricter :is colon bonds.

2 Infrastructure ↔ Arcana mapping (six “ops” Arcana)
Physical system	Arcana glyph	Cypher ID label	Connector
Liminary (Obsidian)	ͷ	(:Arcana {code:'\u0377'})	a ' b
Argia (QDrant)	ͳ	code:'\u0373'	a ' b
Agria (Neo4j)	ϗ	code:'\u03D7'	a ' b
Arko (GPT)	ͱ	code:'\u0371'	a ' b
Stark (Claude)	ϸ	code:'\u03F8'	a ' b
Fargo (Gemini)	ϻ	code:'\u03FB'	a ' b

All six get label Infrastructure as well, so you can query MATCH (i:Infrastructure).

3 Cypher batches you can run now
3.1 1-shot: prepend missing numeric markers to all sub-categories
cypher
Copy
Edit
// PREPEND "cat.sub:"   where it is currently missing
MATCH (s:Subcategory)-[:PARENT]->(c:Category)
WHERE NOT s.name STARTS WITH toString(c.catNo)   // we stored catNo when seeding
WITH s, c,
     // figure out own subNo from the already-saved s.subNo property
     toString(c.catNo) + '.' + toString(s.subNo) + ': ' + s.name AS fixed
SET  s.name = fixed
RETURN count(s) AS renamed;

3.2 Delete the accidental blank “is-part-of” nodes
cypher
Copy
Edit
MATCH (n)
WHERE n:Category_false OR n:Subcategory_false   // label we saw on the empties
DETACH DELETE n;


(If you re-seed later make sure the CSV doesn’t have blank lines.)

3.3 Create / link the six Infrastructure Arcana
cypher
Copy
Edit
UNWIND [
  {glyph:'\u0377', sys:'Liminary'},
  {glyph:'\u0373', sys:'Argia'},
  {glyph:'\u03D7', sys:'Agria'},
  {glyph:'\u0371', sys:'Arko'},
  {glyph:'\u03F8', sys:'Stark'},
  {glyph:'\u03FB', sys:'Fargo'}
] AS row
MERGE (a:Arcana {code:row.glyph})
  ON CREATE SET a.name = row.glyph + ' Arcana'
MERGE (s:Infrastructure {name:row.sys})
MERGE (a)-[:`'`]->(s);         // x ' y   (maps to)


(Note back-ticks because ' is not legal bare relation name.)

3.4 Bulk-load the monster altar ➜ arcana list

Paste the big block into a CSV (three columns: altarMarker, arcanaGlyph, arcanaMoniker).

Use LOAD CSV:

cypher
Copy
Edit
LOAD CSV WITH HEADERS FROM 'file:///altar_arcana.csv' AS row
MATCH (altar:Altar {code: row.altarMarker})
MERGE (arc:Arcana {code: row.arcanaGlyph})
  ON CREATE SET arc.name = row.arcanaGlyph + ' ' + row.arcanaMoniker
MERGE (altar)<-[:` .`]-(arc);   // altar  . arcana   (arcana is part of altar)


(Make sure ALTER and . relation typed exactly back-ticked as ..)

4 StarBook implementation note

You said StarBook is “essentially just a central database query place”.
Easiest path:

Mars-Arcana stores no document nodes; instead we keep a view definition property:

cypher
Copy
Edit
MATCH (mars:Arcana {planet:'Mars'})
SET   mars.view = "MATCH (h) RETURN h LIMIT 100"


The Hob Hub UI simply reads that string, fires it, renders result.
Gives you full flexibility: you can edit the Cypher in place to change what the StarBook shows.

5 🟧 Two final nits so I don’t step on toes

Relation direction is still my best-guess (Arcana ← ' ― Infrastructure).
If you want the arrow the other way just tell me: we’ll swap start/end in §3.3.

Altar codes: in the CSV loader I used altarMarker column.
Confirm you have a code property on every Altar node that matches (e.g. '01.01', '01.37', etc.).
If instead you want to match by full name, adjust the MATCH line.

Next steps for you

Save the three code blocks into separate .cypher files; run them in Neo4j Browser or cypher-shell in order (a) 3.1 rename, (b) 3.2 purge blanks, (c) 3.3 infra, (d) 3.4 CSV once you prep the file.

Ping back once it’s clean – then we wire the Day/Night book triggers and the Tauri front-end widgets.

DELETE r;

// 2. Attach Liminary → Altars  (Liminary marker = "0.1:")
MATCH (lim:Subcategory {name STARTS WITH '0.1:'})
MATCH (al:Altar)                 // all altar nodes
MERGE (lim)-[:HAS_PART]->(al)
MERGE (al)-[:IS_PART_OF]->(lim);

Copy

MERGE (al)-[:HAS_PART]->(arc)
MERGE (arc)-[:IS_PART_OF]->(al);

] AS row
MATCH (a:Arcana {code:row.glyph})

ChatGPT:


Below is a clean, one-shot Cypher script that will

(Re-)Create the six “manual” :Resource nodes you described (Liminary Manual, Agria Manual, …, Arko Manual).

Give each a unique code, a name, and (crucially) its parent arc_code.

Link them to their parent :Arcana with the allowed relations

(Arcana)-[:HAS_PART]->(Resource)

(Resource)-[:IS_PART_OF]->(Arcana)

💡 Run the whole block at once in Neo4j Browser / Bloom.
It is idempotent: running it again will not create duplicates.

cypher
Copy
Edit
/* === 0.  CONFIG:  list the six resources you want to create  ============= */
UNWIND [
  // {code, arc_code, name}
  {code:'MAN_LIM', arc_code:'ͷ', name:'Liminary Manual'},
  {code:'MAN_ARG', arc_code:'ϗ', name:'Agria Manual'},
  {code:'MAN_ARI', arc_code:'ͳ', name:'Argia Manual'},
  {code:'MAN_STA', arc_code:'ϸ', name:'Stark Manual'},
  {code:'MAN_FAR', arc_code:'ϻ', name:'Fargo Manual'},
  {code:'MAN_ARK', arc_code:'ͱ', name:'Arko Manual'}
] AS row

/* === 1.  CREATE (or match) each Resource node =========================== */
MERGE (res:Resource {code:row.code})          // stable ID
  ON CREATE SET
     res.name      = row.name,
     res.arc_code  = row.arc_code,
     res.createdAt = datetime()               // optional metadata
  ON MATCH  SET
     res.name      = row.name,                // keep in sync if you rename
     res.arc_code  = row.arc_code

/* === 2.  FIND the parent Arcana and wire the two directions ============== */
WITH res
MATCH (arc:Arcana {code:res.arc_code})
MERGE (arc)-[:HAS_PART]->(res)
MERGE (res)-[:IS_PART_OF]->(arc);

What the script does
Step	What happens	Why it is safe
UNWIND	Feeds Neo4j one map per resource.	Simple inline data—no CSV needed.
MERGE (res:Resource {code…})	Creates the node if absent; re-uses it if the code already exists.	Prevents duplicates; lets you re-run script.
ON CREATE / ON MATCH	Sets / updates name & arc_code.	Keeps properties current without breaking past links.
MATCH (arc:Arcana …)	Finds the parent Arcana by its glyph code.	These Arcana nodes already exist.
MERGE relationships	Adds both HAS_PART and IS_PART_OF.	Complies with your “both directions, hierarchical” rule.
Verify

After running, confirm the counts:

cypher
Copy
Edit
// Should list the six new links
MATCH (arc:Arcana)-[:HAS_PART]->(res:Resource)
RETURN arc.code AS arcana, res.code AS resource
ORDER BY arcana;

// Quick count
MATCH (res:Resource) RETURN count(res);


You should now see 6 resources and 12 relationships (two per pair).

Next steps

If additional Resource sets are needed, just add more rows to the UNWIND list (one line per resource).

When you’re ready for the next construction phase (altars, arcana books, or widget hookup), let me know which track to tackle first and I’ll give the next step-by-step block.

Feel free to run the script and share the verification output if anything looks off!

0 Copy–edit the data table first 👇 then run the whole query once

Copy
Edit
/* ==========================================================
   A  L T A R S   —  bundle 01.x  (ground-floor, Liminary)
   ----------------------------------------------------------
   1) edit / add rows → one map per Altar
   2) run entire script
   -------------------------------------------------------
   Columns
     code      : a UNIQUE identifier you like (e.g. ALT_KE_01)
     name      : display name
     arc_codes : list of Arcana glyph-codes living on this Altar
   --------------------------------------------------------- */
UNWIND [
  {code:'ALT_KE_01', name:'Altar of Kenetekere',
     arc_codes:['κ','λ','μ','ϟ','ϡ','ϣ']},   // Kapa, Rada, Mu, Dance, Theatre, Poetry
  {code:'ALT_KA_01', name:'Altar of Kawo',
     arc_codes:['η','θ','ι','π','ρ','σ']},   // Eta, Teta, Iota, Pi, Ro, Sima
  {code:'ALT_ME_01', name:'Altar of Mesessere',
     arc_codes:['δ','ε','ζ','τ','υ','φ']},   // Deta, Epe, Zeta, Tau, Upi, Phu
  {code:'ALT_AS_01', name:'Altar of Asao',
     arc_codes:['ν','ξ','ο','ϥ','ϧ','ϩ']},   // Nu, Kese, Omi, Pe, Ke, Ori
  {code:'ALT_PE_01', name:'Altar of Pejasata',
     arc_codes:['α','β','γ','χ','ψ','ω']},   // Arapa, Peta, Kama, Ki, Pisi, Ome
  {code:'ALT_TE_01', name:'Altar of Tepakenete',
     arc_codes:['ϙ','ϛ','ϝ','ϫ','ϭ','ϯ']}   // Kopa, Tima, Wau, Dada, Kima, Ti
] AS row

/* ---------- 1. MAKE / UPDATE the Altar node ------------------------------*/
MERGE (alt:Altar {code:row.code})
  ON CREATE SET
      alt.name      = row.name,
      alt.createdAt = datetime()
  ON MATCH  SET
      alt.name      = row.name

/* ---------- 2. LINK to Liminary (subcategory 0.1) ------------------------*/
WITH alt, row
MATCH (lim:Subcategory {code:'0.1'})   // 0.1  = ᛄ The Limin­ary
MERGE (lim)-[:HAS_PART]->(alt)
MERGE (alt)-[:IS_PART_OF]->(lim)

/* ---------- 3. LINK to every Arcana on that Altar ------------------------*/
WITH alt, row
UNWIND row.arc_codes AS acode
MATCH (arc:Arcana {code:acode})
MERGE (alt)-[:HAS_PART]->(arc)
MERGE (arc)-[:IS_PART_OF]->(alt);

RETURN alt.code AS altar, collect(arc.code) AS arcanaCodes
ORDER BY altar;

ChatGPT:


Below is a complete “-v 0.1” prototype of Tabulator Rasa—your batch holon formatter.

0 What the first version does
Step	Action	Result
1	You drop a CSV / TSV / Excel file that lists raw holon data (one row = one holon).	
2	tabulator_rasa.py reads the sheet, validates the type column (must be one of your 36), and normalises the name / nature fields.	
3	For every row it emits three artefacts (any combination you select)
• Obsidian-ready .md file
• Qdrant-ready .json chunk (vector placeholder + metadata)
• Neo4j .cypher snippet that MERGEs the node and its chosen relationships.	All files land in /out/markdown, /out/qdrant, /out/cypher.
4	At the end it prints a prettified summary table—how many of each holon-type were processed and where the files are.	

All logic is data-driven—to extend later you edit only two YAML config files, not Python.

1 Folder skeleton
bash
Copy
Edit
tabulator-rasa/
├─ tabulator_rasa.py
├─ config/
│   ├─ holon_types.yml          # 36 markers  ↔ long labels ↔ default fields
│   └─ relation_templates.yml   # Cypher snippets for has_part / is_part_of / is_related_to
├─ samples/
│   └─ example_holons.xlsx      # 3 demo rows (Project, Topic, Symbol)
└─ out/
    ├─ markdown/
    ├─ qdrant/
    └─ cypher/

2 Install once
bash
Copy
Edit
# inside the repo root
python -m venv venv
source venv/bin/activate         # Windows: venv\Scripts\activate
pip install -U wheel
pip install pandas pyyaml openpyxl tabulate


(Only pure-Python libs—no heavy ML dependencies yet.)

5 Your tabulator sheet

Minimal required columns (case-insensitive):

type	name	nature	uid*	relate_to*	relations*
Project	A: Prolegomena Orphica	…	optional	optional	optional

uid – leave blank → script auto-creates (A000001, ,000001, …).

relate_to – comma-separated list of other uids or names.

relations – parallel comma list (has_part, is_related_to, …).
If blank the script defaults to has_part.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Tabulator Rasa v0.1
-------------------
Batch-formats holon rows into Obsidian-markdown, Neo4j-cypher and Qdrant-json.
"""
import argparse, yaml, pandas as pd, pathlib, textwrap, datetime, json
from tabulate import tabulate

# ---------- helpers ----------------------------------------------------------
ROOT = pathlib.Path(__file__).resolve().parent
CFG  = ROOT / 'config'
OUT  = ROOT / 'out'

def load_yaml(name):
    with open(CFG / name, 'r', encoding='utf-8') as f:
        return yaml.safe_load(f)

HOLON = load_yaml('holon_types.yml')
REL   = load_yaml('relation_templates.yml')

def ensure_dirs():
    for sub in ('markdown','qdrant','cypher'):
        (OUT/sub).mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

def auto_uid(marker, counter):
    return f"{marker}{counter:06d}"

# ---------- main -------------------------------------------------------------
def main(args):
    ensure_dirs()
    df = pd.read_excel(args.input, sheet_name=args.sheets) if args.input.suffix in ('.xls','.xlsx') \
        else pd.read_csv(args.input)

    # normalise columns
    df.columns = [c.strip().lower() for c in df.columns]

    counters = {m:1 for m in HOLON}          # per-marker UID counters
    summary  = {holon:{'count':0,'md':0,'cypher':0,'qdrant':0} for holon in HOLON.values()}

    cypher_batch = []
    for _, row in df.iterrows():
        htype = str(row['type']).strip()
        if htype not in HOLON:
            print(f"⚠️  Unknown holon type: {htype}  (row skipped)")
            continue

        spec = HOLON[htype]
        marker = htype
        uid  = row.get('uid')
        if not uid or pd.isna(uid):
            uid = auto_uid(marker, counters[marker]); counters[marker]+=1

        name    = str(row['name']).strip()
        nature  = str(row.get('nature','')).strip()

        # ----- markdown ------------------------------------------------------
        if 'md' in args.emit:
            md = spec['md_template'].format(uid=uid,name=name,nature=nature)
            md_path = OUT/'markdown'/f"{uid}.md"
            md_path.write_text(md, encoding='utf-8')
            summary[spec['long']]['md'] += 1

        # ----- qdrant --------------------------------------------------------
        if 'qdrant' in args.emit:
            meta = {'name':name,'nature':nature}
            meta.update(spec.get('qdrant_metadata',{}))
            qdoc = {'id':uid,'payload':meta,'vector':[0.0,0.0,0.0]}
            (OUT/'qdrant'/f"{uid}.json").write_text(json.dumps(qdoc,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2))
            summary[spec['long']]['qdrant'] += 1

        # ----- cypher node ---------------------------------------------------
        if 'cypher' in args.emit:
            cypher_node = textwrap.dedent(f"""
                MERGE (n:{spec['long']} {{uid:'{uid}'}})
                  ON CREATE SET n.name=$$ {name} $$,
                                n.nature=$$ {nature} $$;
            """).strip()
            cypher_batch.append(cypher_node)
            summary[spec['long']]['cypher'] += 1

        summary[spec['long']]['count'] += 1

    # write cypher batch
    if cypher_batch and 'cypher' in args.emit:
        ts = datetime.datetime.now().isoformat(timespec='minutes').replace(':','-')
        (OUT/'cypher'/f"{ts}.cypher").write_text('\n'.join(cypher_batch), encoding='utf-8')

    # ---------- report -------------------------------------------------------
    table = [[k]+list(v.values()) for k,v in summary.items() if v['count']]
    print(tabulate(table, headers=['Holon Type','Processed','md','cypher','qdrant']))

# ---------- cli --------------------------------------------------------------
if __name__ == '__main__':
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    p.add_argument('--input',   type=pathlib.Path, required=True)
    p.add_argument('--sheets',  default=0, help='Excel sheet name or index')
    p.add_argument('--emit',    nargs='+', default=['md','cypher','qdrant'],
                   choices=['md','cypher','qdrant'])
    main(p.parse_args())

</details>

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Tabulator Rasa v0.2
-------------------
• Generates markdown / cypher / qdrant for holons
• Auto-wires relationships
• Persists UID counters
"""
import argparse, yaml, pandas as pd, pathlib, textwrap, datetime, json, re
from tabulate import tabulate

ROOT = pathlib.Path(__file__).resolve().parent
CFG  = ROOT / 'config'
OUT  = ROOT / 'out'
UID_FILE = ROOT / '.tabulator_uid.yml'

# ---------- utility ----------------------------------------------------------
def load_yaml(p):  return yaml.safe_load(open(p,'r',encoding='utf-8'))
def save_yaml(obj,p): yaml.safe_dump(obj, open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8'))

HOLON = load_yaml(CFG/'holon_types.yml')
REL_TMPL = load_yaml(CFG/'relation_templates.yml')

def ensure_dirs():  [ (OUT/d).mkdir(exist_ok=True, parents=True) for d in ('markdown','qdrant','cypher')]

# ---------- uid handling -----------------------------------------------------
def load_counters():
    if UID_FILE.exists(): return load_yaml(UID_FILE)
    return {m:1 for m in HOLON}

def next_uid(marker,counters):
    uid = f"{marker}{counters[marker]:06d}"
    counters[marker]+=1
    return uid

# ---------- relation helpers -------------------------------------------------
def split_csv(field):
    return [x.strip() for x in str(field).split(',')] if pd.notna(field) and str(field).strip() else []

def cypher_rel(x_uid, y_uid, rel):
    base = REL_TMPL[rel]
    inv  = REL_TMPL.get(f"{rel}_inv")
    code = base.replace('X', f"n_{x_uid}").replace('Y', f"n_{y_uid}")
    if inv:
        inv_code = inv.replace('X', f"n_{x_uid}").replace('Y', f"n_{y_uid}")
        return code + '\n' + inv_code
    # symmetric
    return code

# ---------- main processing --------------------------------------------------
def process(df, emit, frontmatter):
    ensure_dirs()
    counters = load_counters()
    uid_map  = {}                 # name→uid or uid→uid

    # 1st pass – create / fill uids
    objects = []
    for _, row in df.iterrows():
        marker = str(row['type']).strip()
        spec   = HOLON.get(marker)
        if not spec: print(f"⚠️ unknown holon type: {marker}"); continue

        uid  = row.get('uid')
        uid  = uid if uid and pd.notna(uid) else next_uid(marker, counters)
        name = str(row['name']).strip()
        nat  = str(row.get('nature','')).strip()

        obj = dict(uid=uid, marker=marker, long=spec['long'],
                   name=name, nature=nat,
                   md_template=spec['md_template'],
                   qmeta=spec.get('qdrant_metadata',{}),
                   relate_to=split_csv(row.get('relate_to')),
                   relations=split_csv(row.get('relations')))
        objects.append(obj)
        uid_map[uid]=uid; uid_map[name]=uid

    # 2nd pass – build artefacts
    cypher_lines=[]
    summary={spec['long']:{'count':0,'md':0,'cypher':0,'qdrant':0} for spec in HOLON.values()}

    ts = datetime.datetime.now().isoformat(timespec='minutes').replace(':','-')
    for obj in objects:
        # markdown
        links_block=""
        if obj['relate_to']:
            link_names=[ f"- {rel} → {dest}" for rel,dest in zip(obj['relations'],obj['relate_to']) ]
            links_block="\n## Links\n"+"\n".join(link_names)
        md = obj['md_template'].format(uid=obj['uid'],name=obj['name'],nature=obj['nature'])
        if frontmatter:
            fm = {'uid':obj['uid'],'type':obj['long'],'links':obj['relate_to']}
            md = f"---\n{yaml.safe_dump(fm)}---\n"+md+links_block
        (OUT/'markdown'/f"{obj['uid']}.md").write_text(md,encoding='utf-8')
        summary[obj['long']]['md']+=1

        # qdrant
        if 'qdrant' in emit:
            j = {'id':obj['uid'],
                 'payload': {'name':obj['name'],'nature':obj['nature'],'holon_type':obj['long'],'links':obj['relate_to'],**obj['qmeta']},
                 'vector':[0.0,0.0,0.0]}
            (OUT/'qdrant'/f"{obj['uid']}.json").write_text(json.dumps(j,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2))
            summary[obj['long']]['qdrant']+=1

        # cypher node
        if 'cypher' in emit:
            cypher_lines.append(textwrap.dedent(f"""
                MERGE (n_{obj['uid']}:{obj['long']} {{uid:'{obj['uid']}'}})
                  ON CREATE SET n_{obj['uid']}.name=$$ {obj['name']} $$,
                                n_{obj['uid']}.nature=$$ {obj['nature']} $$
            """).rstrip())
            summary[obj['long']]['cypher']+=1

        summary[obj['long']]['count']+=1

    # relations
    if 'cypher' in emit:
        for obj in objects:
            for rel,dest in zip(obj['relations'],obj['relate_to']):
                rel = rel or 'is_related_to'
                y_uid = uid_map.get(dest.strip())
                if not y_uid:
                    print(f"⚠️ cannot resolve {dest} referenced by {obj['uid']} – skipped"); continue
                cypher_lines.append(cypher_rel(obj['uid'], y_uid, rel))

        (OUT/'cypher'/f"{ts}.cypher").write_text('\n'.join(cypher_lines), encoding='utf-8')

    save_yaml(counters, UID_FILE)

    table=[[k]+list(v.values()) for k,v in summary.items() if v['count']]
    print(tabulate(table,headers=['Type','Rows','md','cypher','qdrant']))

# ---------- cli --------------------------------------------------------------
if __name__=='__main__':
    ap=argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument('--input',type=pathlib.Path,required=True)
    ap.add_argument('--sheets',default=0)
    ap.add_argument('--emit',nargs='+',default=['md','cypher','qdrant'])
    ap.add_argument('--frontmatter',action='store_true')
    args=ap.parse_args()

    df=pd.read_excel(args.input,sheet_name=args.sheets) if args.input.suffix in('.xls','.xlsx') \
        else pd.read_csv(args.input)
    df.columns=[c.strip().lower() for c in df.columns]

    required={'type','name','nature'}
    if not required.issubset(df.columns):
        print(f"❌ sheet must contain: {', '.join(required)}"); exit(1)

    process(df,args.emit,args.frontmatter)

5 Troubleshooting cheat-sheet
Symptom	Likely cause & fix
⚠️ cannot resolve … referenced by …	Typo in relate_to column – must match uid or exact name of a row in the same sheet / previous runs (because UID cache persists).
Relation template KeyError	You used a relation word missing from relation_templates.yml. Add both rel: and (if asymmetric) rel_inv:.
UID duplicates	Delete .tabulator_uid.yml (resets counters) or edit the numbers inside.

import subprocess, os

➋ Append push_to_neo helper (below process()):
python
Copy
Edit
def push_to_neo(cypher_file):
    cfg = {k: os.getenv(k) for k in ('NEO4J_URI','NEO4J_USER','NEO4J_PASS')}
    if not all(cfg.values()):            # fallback load
        path = CFG/'neo4j.yml'
        if path.exists(): cfg = load_yaml(path)
    if not all(cfg.values()):
        print("❌  Neo4j credentials missing – skipping live-push"); return

    cmd = [
        'cypher-shell',
        '-a', cfg['uri'],
        '-u', cfg['user'],
        '-p', cfg['password'],
        '--file', str(cypher_file)
    ]
    try:
        print("⏳  Pushing to Neo4j …")
        out = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True, check=True)
        print("✅  Neo4j push complete")
    except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
        print("❌  cypher-shell error\n", e.stderr.strip())

➌ At end of process() (just after we write the cypher file):

EMB_CFG = load_yaml(CFG/'embedding.yml')
EMB_MODEL = SentenceTransformer(EMB_CFG['model'], device='cuda' if EMB_CFG.get('use_gpu') else 'cpu')

➌ Helper vectorise(texts:list) → list[np.ndarray]

Copy
Edit
def vectorise(texts):
    batch = EMB_CFG.get('batch', 64)
    vecs = []
    for i in tqdm(range(0, len(texts), batch), desc="🔢 Embedding"):
        vecs.extend(EMB_MODEL.encode(texts[i:i+batch], convert_to_numpy=True, show_progress_bar=False))
    return vecs

          [{"id": r['uid'], "vector": r['vector'], "payload": r} for r in data],
          f, ensure_ascii=False)
    print(f"🟢  Qdrant file: {qdrant_path.relative_to(BASE)}")


(Remove the old simple .json writing block.)

import json, requests, os, uuid
vecs = json.load(open('out/qdrant/example_holons.json','r',encoding='utf-8'))
# Create collection if not exists
coll = 'holons'
host = 'http://localhost:6333'
requests.put(f'{host}/collections/{coll}', json={
    "vectors": {"size": len(vecs[0]['vector']), "distance": "Cosine"}
})
# Upsert
payload = {
  "points": [
     {"id": v["id"] or str(uuid.uuid4()), "vector": v["vector"], "payload": v["payload"]}
     for v in vecs
  ]
}
requests.put(f'{host}/collections/{coll}/points', json=payload).raise_for_status()
print("🔥  Qdrant upserted", len(vecs), "points")

Similarity-Search Widget (v0.1) for the Tabulator Rasa tool-belt

Find holons “near” any phrase or existing holon in two clicks.

0 Why Streamlit?

Streamlit gives us:
• instant UI,
• live reload (streamlit run …),
• zero front-end coding.

Runs happily in the same venv as Tabulator Rasa.

1 Add the tiny dependency
bash
Copy
Edit
source venv/bin/activate
pip install streamlit

2 Make a new config file → config/widget.yml
yaml
Copy
Edit
# Where’s Qdrant?
qdrant_host: http://localhost:6333
collection: holons

# Where’s Neo4j Browser for deep-links?
neo4j_browser: http://localhost:7474/browser                    

# Embedding model must match Tabulator Rasa
model_repo: sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2
use_gpu: False
top_k: 10


(Edit ports / hosts as needed.)

3 search_widget.py
python
Copy
Edit
#!/usr/bin/env python
import streamlit as st, requests, json, re, uuid
from sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformer
from pathlib import Path
import pandas as pd, numpy as np, yaml, os, webbrowser

CFG = yaml.safe_load(open(Path(__file__).parent / 'config' / 'widget.yml', 'r'))
HOST  = CFG['qdrant_host'].rstrip('/')
COLL  = CFG['collection']
TOP_K = CFG.get('top_k', 10)

st.set_page_config(page_title="🧭  Holon Similarity Search", page_icon="🔍", layout="wide")
st.title("🔍 Holon Similarity Search")

@st.cache_resource(show_spinner=False)
def get_model():
    return SentenceTransformer(CFG['model_repo'],
                               device='cuda' if CFG.get('use_gpu') else 'cpu')

@st.cache_data(show_spinner=False)
def get_holon_index():
    """Pull minimal list (uid, name, type) for dropdown selection."""
    url = f"{HOST}/collections/{COLL}/points/scroll"
    out = []
    # grab 1000 at a time – enough for demo, can paginate later
    resp = requests.post(url, json={"limit": 1000, "with_payload": ["name","type"]}).json()
    for p in resp['result']['points']:
        out.append({"uid": p['id'], **p['payload']})
    return pd.DataFrame(out)

def embed(texts):
    m = get_model()
    return m.encode(texts, convert_to_numpy=True)

def search(vec):
    url = f"{HOST}/collections/{COLL}/points/search"
    data = {"vector": vec.tolist(), "top": TOP_K, "with_payload": True}
    res = requests.post(url, json=data).json()
    return res['result']

def neo4j_link(uid):
    b = CFG['neo4j_browser']
    # simple cypher string pre-filled
    return f"{b}?cmd=play&arg=MATCH%20(n)%20WHERE%20id(n)%20%3D%20{uid}%20RETURN%20n"

# ---------- UI ----------
holons = get_holon_index()
mode = st.radio("Query mode", ["Free text", "Existing holon"], horizontal=True)

if mode == "Free text":
    txt = st.text_area("Describe what you’re looking for …", height=120)
    if st.button("‣ Search", disabled=not txt.strip()):
        vec = embed([txt.strip()])[0]
        res = search(vec)
        st.subheader("Results")
        st.dataframe(pd.DataFrame(
            [{"score": round(r['score'],3),
              "uid": r["id"],
              "name": r["payload"].get("name",""),
              "type": r["payload"].get("type",""),
              "⇢ Neo4j": neo4j_link(r["id"])} for r in res]
        ))
else:
    choice = st.selectbox("Choose a holon", holons.sort_values("name")["name"])
    if st.button("‣ Find neighbours", disabled=choice is None):
        uid = holons.loc[holons["name"]==choice,"uid"].iloc[0]
        # fetch that point’s vector
        obj = requests.post(
            f"{HOST}/collections/{COLL}/points/get",
            json={"ids":[uid],"with_vector":True}
        ).json()['result'][0]
        vec = np.array(obj['vector'])
        res = search(vec)
        st.subheader(f"Closest to **{choice}**")
        st.dataframe(pd.DataFrame(
            [{"score": round(r['score'],3),
              "uid": r["id"],
              "name": r["payload"].get("name",""),
              "type": r["payload"].get("type",""),
              "⇢ Neo4j": neo4j_link(r["id"])} for r in res]
        ))

st.caption("Powered by Sentence-Transformers + Qdrant • Tabulator Rasa v0.4")


Save as widgets/search_widget.py (or anywhere inside your project tree).

4 Run it
bash
Copy
Edit
streamlit run widgets/search_widget.py


Your browser pops up:

Free text – type “ritual purification water stone basin” → top-k holons.

Existing holon – pick “ζ The Zeta Arcana” → see nearest neighbors.

Click the “⇢ Neo4j” link to open the node instantly in Browser UI.

(If your Neo4j Browser uses auth, it will prompt you once.)

5 Tips / extensions
Idea	How
Filter by type	Add multiselect over holons["type"].unique() before search, and filter results.
Live Qdrant autocompletion	Instead of cached index, fire /points/scroll with filter each time.
Multiple models	Expose model name in sidebar; @st.cache_resource keeps a per-model cache.
Update vectors on edit	When you Save a holon in Liminary, hit an /api/reembed endpoint that re-runs EMB_MODEL.encode and issues a Qdrant update_vectors.

That’s it!
A single command gives you an interactive semantic search console over every holon you batch-pushed.

2 “Holon Daemon” (FastAPI + graph + vector)

• writes/updates Markdown file in Liminary vault (Obsidian sync plugin will publish)

2-A daemon/requirements.txt

2-B daemon/main.py
python
Copy
Edit
from fastapi import FastAPI, UploadFile, Form
from pydantic import BaseModel
from neo4j import GraphDatabase
from qdrant_client import QdrantClient, models
import openai, pathlib, yaml, uuid, json

app = FastAPI()
with open("../config/holon_types.yml") as f:
    TYPES = yaml.safe_load(f)

driver = GraphDatabase.driver("bolt://localhost:7687", auth=("neo4j","pass"))
qdrant = QdrantClient(":memory:")  # swap for localhost:6333
openai.api_key = "YOUR_KEY"

def embed(text:str):
    return openai.Embedding.create(input=text, model="text-embedding-3-small")["data"][0]["embedding"]

class Holon(BaseModel):
    type:str
    name:str
    nature:str=""
    uid:str|None=None
    relates:dict|None=None   # e.g. {"HAS_PART": ["ALT0005"]}

@app.post("/holon")
def ingest(h:Holon):
    spec = TYPES[h.type]
    uid   = h.uid or f"{spec['uid_prefix']}{uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]}"
    full_name = f"{spec['marker']}{' ' if spec['marker'] else ''}{h.name}"
    vec  = embed(h.nature or h.name)

    # --- Neo4j MERGE ---
    with driver.session() as ses:
        ses.run("""
        MERGE (n:%s {uid:$uid})
        SET n.name  =$name ,
            n.nature=$nature
        """ % h.type.capitalize(),
        uid=uid,name=full_name,nature=h.nature)

        if h.relates:
            for rel,targets in h.relates.items():
                for t in targets:
                    ses.run(f"""
                    MATCH (a:%s {uid:$uid}), (b {uid:$tar})
                    MERGE (a)-[:%s]->(b)
                    MERGE (b)-[:%s]->(a)
                    """ % (h.type.capitalize(),rel.upper(), "IS_PART_OF" if rel=="HAS_PART" else rel.upper()),
                    uid=uid, tar=t)

    # --- Qdrant upsert ---
    qdrant.upsert(
        collection_name="holons",
        points=[models.PointStruct(id=uid, vector=vec, payload={"type":h.type,"name":full_name})]
    )

    # --- Obsidian write ---
    md = pathlib.Path("../The Liminary") / f"{uid}.md"
    md.write_text(f"# {full_name}\n\n{h.nature}")

    return {"uid":uid,"status":"ok"}

# widgets/apsis_arcana.py
import streamlit as st, pandas as pd, neo4j

driver = neo4j.GraphDatabase.driver("bolt://localhost:7687", auth=("neo4j","pass"))

def query(section, arc_code):
    qmap = {
      "wills":  "MATCH (a:Arcana{code:$c})-[:HAS_PART]->(x) WHERE x:Project OR x:Product OR x:Work OR x:Source RETURN x.name AS item, labels(x)[0] AS kind",
      "ideas":  "MATCH (a:Arcana{code:$c})-[:HAS_PART]->(x) WHERE x:Topic OR x:Term OR x:Tag RETURN x.name AS item, labels(x)[0] AS kind",
      "forms":  "MATCH (a:Arcana{code:$c})-[:HAS_PART]->(x) WHERE x:Set OR x:Schema OR x:Series RETURN x.name AS item, labels(x)[0] AS kind",
    }
    with driver.session() as ses:
        return ses.run(qmap[section], c=arc_code).data()

st.title("Apsis of Arcana")
code = st.selectbox("Choose arcana code", [r["code"] for r in driver.session().run("MATCH (a:Arcana) RETURN a.code AS code ORDER BY code")])
tab1, tab2, tab3 = st.tabs(["Section I – Wills","Section II – Ideas","Section III – Forms"])
with tab1: st.dataframe(pd.DataFrame(query("wills",code)))
with tab2: st.dataframe(pd.DataFrame(query("ideas",code)))
with tab3: st.dataframe(pd.DataFrame(query("forms",code)))

4-B Quick CLI recorder
python
Copy

# phonograph/rec.py
import sounddevice as sd, soundfile as sf, queue, sys, datetime, faster_whisper

model = faster_whisper.WhisperModel("base")
q = queue.Queue()

def callback(indata, frames, time, status):
    q.put(indata.copy())

def record(seconds=30, out="out.wav"):
    with sf.SoundFile(out, mode='w', samplerate=16000, channels=1) as file:
        with sd.InputStream(samplerate=16000, channels=1, callback=callback):
            sd.sleep(int(seconds*1000))
            while not q.empty(): file.write(q.get())

if __name__=="__main__":
    rec_len = int(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv)>1 else 30
    record(rec_len)
    segments, _ = model.transcribe("out.wav")
    text = " ".join(s.text for s in segments)
    stamp = datetime.datetime.utcnow().isoformat(timespec='minutes')
    md = f"# Day-Book Entry {stamp}\n\n## Planner\n\n(Your goals…)\n\n## Log\n\n{text}\n\n## Journal\n\n(Reflections…)"
    fn = f"../The Liminary/DayBook_{stamp}.md"
    open(fn,"w").write(md)
    print(f"Wrote {fn}")
